## P4 - `cp -p` backup drops attributes that the base `mv` preserved

**Location:** [nodes/frr/frr.go:271-282](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/7d2236b24bf6dcdd313993b4d8b4029e6d2a18e3/nodes/frr/frr.go#L271-L282)

**Problem:** The base script published the backup with `mv`, so `/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab` was the original inode with every attribute intact. The new script builds the backup with `cp -p`, which preserves only mode, ownership, and timestamps; extended attributes such as file capabilities &#40;`security.capability`&#41; and ACLs are not preserved by `cp -p`, and a symlinked `vtysh` would additionally be dereferenced into a regular file.

**Trigger:** Only an image whose `vtysh` binary carries file capabilities, ACLs, or is a symlink. The current FRR containerlab and release images do not set capabilities on `vtysh`, so the impact is latent on today&#39;s images.

**Impact:** The installed wrapper `exec`s `/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab`, so any lost capability or ACL would apply to every wrapped `vtysh -w` invocation on such an image.

**Suggested correction:** Replace `cp -p` with `cp -a`, which preserves mode, ownership, timestamps, and xattrs at the same cost, or keep `cp -p` and mark the deliberate tradeoff with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling.


## P4 - `CHANGE:` comment markers are review-session artifacts

**Location:** [nodes/frr/frr.go:271-273](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/7d2236b24bf6dcdd313993b4d8b4029e6d2a18e3/nodes/frr/frr.go#L271-L273)

**Problem:** The comments introduced by this PR in [`nodes/frr/frr.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/7d2236b24bf6dcdd313993b4d8b4029e6d2a18e3/nodes/frr/frr.go#L1-L1) and [`nodes/frr/frr_save_wrapper_test.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/7d2236b24bf6dcdd313993b4d8b4029e6d2a18e3/nodes/frr/frr_save_wrapper_test.go#L1-L1) begin with `CHANGE:`, which addresses a specific review iteration rather than future readers. The explanatory text itself is accurate and useful.

**Trigger:** Every future reader of `installConfigSaveWrapper` or the new test file encounters the marker with no change to relate it to once the PR merges.

**Impact:** Minor reader confusion and avoidable comment churn in future diffs; no functional effect.

**Suggested correction:** Drop the `CHANGE:` prefixes in [`nodes/frr/frr.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/7d2236b24bf6dcdd313993b4d8b4029e6d2a18e3/nodes/frr/frr.go#L1-L1) lines 271-273 and [`nodes/frr/frr_save_wrapper_test.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/7d2236b24bf6dcdd313993b4d8b4029e6d2a18e3/nodes/frr/frr_save_wrapper_test.go#L1-L1) lines 25-26 and keep the remaining explanatory text unchanged.


## Coverage

- Source-only review&#58; the changed install script in [`nodes/frr/frr.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/7d2236b24bf6dcdd313993b4d8b4029e6d2a18e3/nodes/frr/frr.go#L1-L1), the new [`nodes/frr/frr_save_wrapper_test.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/7d2236b24bf6dcdd313993b4d8b4029e6d2a18e3/nodes/frr/frr_save_wrapper_test.go#L1-L1), the pre-existing `TestPostDeployConfigSaveWrapper` in [`nodes/frr/frr_test.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/7d2236b24bf6dcdd313993b4d8b4029e6d2a18e3/nodes/frr/frr_test.go#L1-L1), [`docs/manual/kinds/frr.md`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/7d2236b24bf6dcdd313993b4d8b4029e6d2a18e3/docs/manual/kinds/frr.md#L1-L1), and [`schemas/clab.schema.json`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/7d2236b24bf6dcdd313993b4d8b4029e6d2a18e3/schemas/clab.schema.json#L1-L1) were inspected; no tests or containers were executed, so runtime behavior is unverified.
- The atomicity design was traced statically&#58; the backup is published by `mv` from a `mktemp` file and the wrapper likewise, `/usr/bin/vtysh` is never absent on the happy path, and the `EXIT` trap cleans both temporaries.
- Dependencies and APIs check out&#58; `github.com/google/go-cmp/cmp` is already a direct dependency in [`go.mod`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/7d2236b24bf6dcdd313993b4d8b4029e6d2a18e3/go.mod#L1-L1), and `NewExecResult`/`SetReturnCode`/`SetStdErr` match existing usage in [`exec/exec.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/7d2236b24bf6dcdd313993b4d8b4029e6d2a18e3/exec/exec.go#L1-L1).
- Leftover `vtysh.containerlab.XXXXXX` and `vtysh.wrapper.XXXXXX` files after a `SIGKILL`ed install are accepted by design and asserted by the retry test; they never become the backup, so no finding was raised.
- The pre-existing hazard of backing up an already-wrapped `vtysh` when `vtysh.containerlab` is missing exists identically in the base and was not worsened by this PR.
- No release-note entry was added; [`.cursor/rules/release-note-review.mdc`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/7d2236b24bf6dcdd313993b4d8b4029e6d2a18e3/.cursor/rules/release-note-review.mdc#L1-L1) only mandates inspection for changes under `docs/rn/`, which this PR does not touch, so this is noted only as a possible maintainer follow-up.

72: +    local status=0
73: +    "$FRR_TEST_VTYSH" -b 'two words' > "$FRR_TEST_OUTPUT" 2>&1 || status=$?
74: +    builtin printf '%s:%s\n' "$1" "$status" >> "$FRR_TEST_PROBES"
75: +}
76: +cp() {
77: +    case "$FRR_TEST_FAULT" in
78: +        copy|copy-interrupted)
79: +            builtin printf 'partial copy' > "${@: -1}"
80: +            [ "$FRR_TEST_FAULT" != copy-interrupted ] || kill -KILL $$
81: +            return 91
82: +            ;;
83: +    esac
84: +    command cp "$@" || return $?
85: +    frr_probe copy
86: +}
87: +mv() {
88: +    local stage=publish
89: +    [ "${@: -1}" != "$FRR_TEST_VTYSH.containerlab" ] || stage=backup
90: +    [ "$FRR_TEST_FAULT" != "$stage" ] || return 91
91: +    command mv "$@" || return $?
92: +    frr_probe "$stage"
93: +}
94: +printf() {
95: +    if [ "$FRR_TEST_FAULT" = write ]; then
96: +        builtin printf 'partial wrapper'
97: +        return 91
98: +    fi
99: +    builtin printf "$@"
100: +}
101: +chmod() {
102: +    [ "$FRR_TEST_FAULT" != chmod ] || return 91
103: +    command chmod "$@" || return $?
104: +    frr_probe chmod
105: +}
106: +`
107: +

109: +	binDir    string
110: +	configDir string
111: +	path      string
112: +	original  []byte
113: +}
114: +
115: +func newSaveWrapperFixture(t *testing.T) saveWrapperFixture {
116: +	t.Helper()
117: +
118: +	dir := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "with spaces")
119: +	f := saveWrapperFixture{
120: +		binDir:    filepath.Join(dir, "bin"),
121: +		configDir: filepath.Join(dir, "config"),
122: +		original:  []byte("#!/bin/sh\nprintf '%s\\n' \"$@\"\n"),
123: +	}
124: +	f.path = filepath.Join(f.binDir, "vtysh")
125: +	for _, path := range []string{f.binDir, f.configDir} {
126: +		if err := os.MkdirAll(path, 0o755); err != nil {
127: +			t.Fatal(err)
128: +		}
129: +	}
130: +
131: +	if err := os.WriteFile(f.path, f.original, 0o751); err != nil {
132: +		t.Fatal(err)
133: +	}
134: +
135: +	t.Setenv("FRR_TEST_VTYSH", f.path)
136: +	t.Setenv("FRR_TEST_PROBES", filepath.Join(dir, "probes"))
137: +	t.Setenv("FRR_TEST_OUTPUT", filepath.Join(dir, "output"))
138: +	t.Setenv("FRR_TEST_FAULT", "")
139: +
140: +	return f
141: +}
142: +
143: +func (f saveWrapperFixture) install(t *testing.T) error {
144: +	t.Helper()
145: +
146: +	n := newTestNode(t, &clabtypes.NodeConfig{ShortName: "router1"})
147: +	rt := clabmocksmockruntime.NewMockContainerRuntime(gomock.NewController(t))
148: +	n.WithRuntime(rt)
149: +	rt.EXPECT().Exec(gomock.Any(), gomock.Any(), gomock.Any()).DoAndReturn(
150: +		func(ctx context.Context, _ string, cmd *clabexec.ExecCmd) (*clabexec.ExecResult, error) {
151: +			args := append([]string(nil), cmd.GetCmd()...)
152: +			for _, i := range []int{2, 4} {
153: +				args[i] = strings.ReplaceAll(args[i], "/usr/bin", f.binDir)
154: +				args[i] = strings.ReplaceAll(args[i], etcFRR, f.configDir)
155: +			}
156: +
157: +			args[2] = saveWrapperInterposers + args[2]
158: +			process := exec.CommandContext(ctx, args[0], args[1:]...)
159: +			process.WaitDelay = time.Second
160: +			out, err := process.CombinedOutput()
161: +			res := clabexec.NewExecResult(cmd)
162: +			if err != nil {
163: +				if exit, ok := err.(*exec.ExitError); ok {
164: +					res.SetReturnCode(exit.ExitCode())
165: +					res.SetStdErr(out)
166: +
167: +					return res, nil
168: +				}
169: +
170: +				return nil, err
171: +			}
172: +
173: +			return res, nil
174: +		})
175: +
176: +	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(t.Context(), 5*time.Second)
177: +	defer cancel()
178: +
179: +	return n.installConfigSaveWrapper(ctx)
180: +}

182: +func (f saveWrapperFixture) assertCallable(t *testing.T) {
183: +	t.Helper()
184: +
185: +	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(t.Context(), 5*time.Second)
186: +	defer cancel()
187: +

189: +	cmd.WaitDelay = time.Second

192: +		t.Fatalf("canonical vtysh is unavailable: output=%q error=%v", out, err)
193: +	}
194: +}

197: +	t.Helper()
198: +
199: +	data, err := os.ReadFile(f.path + ".containerlab")
200: +	if err != nil || string(data) != string(f.original) {
201: +		t.Fatalf("original backup changed or incomplete: %q, error=%v", data, err)
202: +	}
203: +}

205: +func (f saveWrapperFixture) assertNoTemporaryFiles(t *testing.T) {
206: +	t.Helper()
207: +
208: +	if files := f.temporaryFiles(t); files != "" {
209: +		t.Errorf("temporary installation files were not cleaned up: %s", files)
210: +	}
211: +}

213: +func (f saveWrapperFixture) temporaryFiles(t *testing.T) string {
214: +	t.Helper()
215: +
216: +	files, err := os.ReadDir(f.binDir)
217: +	if err != nil {
218: +		t.Fatal(err)
219: +	}
220: +
221: +	var names []string
222: +	for _, file := range files {
223: +		if file.Name() != "vtysh" && file.Name() != "vtysh.containerlab" {
224: +			names = append(names, file.Name())
225: +		}
226: +	}
227: +
228: +	return strings.Join(names, "\n")
229: +}

231: +func TestConfigSaveWrapperRemainsExecutableDuringInstall(t *testing.T) {
232: +	f := newSaveWrapperFixture(t)
233: +	for range 2 {
234: +		if err := f.install(t); err != nil {
235: +			t.Fatal(err)
236: +		}
237: +
238: +		f.assertCallable(t)
239: +		f.assertBackup(t)
240: +		f.assertNoTemporaryFiles(t)
241: +	}
242: +

244: +	if err != nil {
245: +		t.Fatal(err)
246: +	}
247: +
248: +	want := []string{"copy:0", "backup:0", "chmod:0", "publish:0", "chmod:0", "publish:0"}
249: +	if diff := cmp.Diff(want, strings.Fields(string(data))); diff != "" {
250: +		t.Errorf("vtysh probes between installation operations (-want +got):\n%s", diff)
251: +	}
252: +}
253: +
254: +func TestConfigSaveWrapperInstallationFailureAndRetry(t *testing.T) {
255: +	for _, fault := range []string{"copy", "copy-interrupted", "backup", "write", "chmod", "publish"} {
256: +		t.Run(fault, func(t *testing.T) {
257: +			f := newSaveWrapperFixture(t)
258: +			t.Setenv("FRR_TEST_FAULT", fault)
259: +			if err := f.install(t); err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), "router1") {
260: +				t.Fatalf("installation error = %v, want a node-specific failure", err)
261: +			}
262: +
263: +			f.assertCallable(t)
264: +			switch fault {
265: +			case "copy", "copy-interrupted", "backup":
266: +				if _, err := os.Stat(f.path + ".containerlab"); !os.IsNotExist(err) {
267: +					t.Fatalf("incomplete backup was published: %v", err)
268: +				}
269: +			default:
270: +				f.assertBackup(t)
271: +			}
272: +
273: +			// SIGKILL cannot run cleanup; abandoned temporary files must not
274: +			// become the original backup or interfere with the next attempt.
275: +			if fault != "copy-interrupted" {
276: +				f.assertNoTemporaryFiles(t)
277: +			}
278: +
279: +			leftovers := f.temporaryFiles(t)
280: +			if fault == "copy-interrupted" && leftovers == "" {
281: +				t.Fatal("interrupted-copy fixture did not leave a temporary copy")
282: +			}
283: +
284: +			t.Setenv("FRR_TEST_FAULT", "")
285: +			if err := f.install(t); err != nil {
286: +				t.Fatalf("retry failed: %v", err)
287: +			}
288: +
289: +			f.assertCallable(t)
290: +			f.assertBackup(t)
291: +			if fault != "copy-interrupted" {
292: +				f.assertNoTemporaryFiles(t)
293: +			} else if got := f.temporaryFiles(t); got != leftovers {
294: +				t.Errorf(
295: +					"retry changed abandoned temporary files: before=%q after=%q",
296: +					leftovers,
297: +					got,
298: +				)
299: +			}
300: +		})
301: +	}
302: +}
303: +
304: +func TestConfigSaveWrapperReinstallationFailure(t *testing.T) {
305: +	for _, fault := range []string{"write", "chmod", "publish"} {
306: +		t.Run(fault, func(t *testing.T) {
307: +			f := newSaveWrapperFixture(t)
308: +			if err := f.install(t); err != nil {
309: +				t.Fatal(err)
310: +			}
311: +
312: +			before, err := os.ReadFile(f.path)
313: +			if err != nil {
314: +				t.Fatal(err)
315: +			}
316: +
317: +			t.Setenv("FRR_TEST_FAULT", fault)
318: +			if err := f.install(t); err == nil {
319: +				t.Fatal("reinstallation unexpectedly succeeded")
320: +			}
321: +
322: +			after, err := os.ReadFile(f.path)
323: +			if err != nil || string(after) != string(before) {
324: +				t.Fatalf("failed reinstall changed the working wrapper: %v", err)
325: +			}
326: +
327: +			f.assertCallable(t)
328: +			f.assertBackup(t)
329: +			f.assertNoTemporaryFiles(t)
330: +		})
331: +	}
332: +}

334: +func TestConfigSaveWrapperPreservesOriginalMetadata(t *testing.T) {
335: +	f := newSaveWrapperFixture(t)
336: +	// Root can also exercise preservation of ownership different from the
337: +	// installer, as with the frrvty group in the container image.
338: +	if os.Geteuid() == 0 {
339: +		if err := os.Chown(f.path, 12345, 12346); err != nil {
340: +			t.Fatal(err)
341: +		}
342: +	}
343: +
344: +	// The image's BusyBox cp preserves timestamps at whole-second precision,
345: +	// which also matches the packaged executable's timestamp.
346: +	modified := time.Unix(1_700_000_000, 0)
347: +	if err := os.Chtimes(f.path, modified, modified); err != nil {
348: +		t.Fatal(err)
349: +	}
350: +
351: +	before, err := os.Stat(f.path)
352: +	if err != nil {
353: +		t.Fatal(err)
354: +	}
355: +
356: +	if err := f.install(t); err != nil {
357: +		t.Fatal(err)
358: +	}
359: +
360: +	after, err := os.Stat(f.path + ".containerlab")
361: +	if err != nil {
362: +		t.Fatal(err)
363: +	}
364: +
365: +	if before.Mode() != after.Mode() || !before.ModTime().Equal(after.ModTime()) {
366: +		t.Errorf("backup mode/time changed: before=%v %v after=%v %v",
367: +			before.Mode(), before.ModTime(), after.Mode(), after.ModTime())
368: +	}
369: +
370: +	oldStat, oldOK := before.Sys().(*syscall.Stat_t)
371: +	newStat, newOK := after.Sys().(*syscall.Stat_t)
372: +	if !oldOK || !newOK || oldStat.Uid != newStat.Uid || oldStat.Gid != newStat.Gid {
373: +		t.Error("backup ownership changed or could not be inspected")
374: +	}
375: +}

45: 	// authzKeysGuard skips a user the image does not have. admin ships only in
46: 	// the containerlab flavour, so a plain release image still gets root's keys
47: 	// instead of an error.
48: 	authzKeysGuard = `id -u "$user" >/dev/null 2>&1 || continue`

50: 	// authzKeysChown hands the file to the user who logs in with it; sshd
51: 	// rejects an authorized_keys that user does not own.

53: 
54: 	// authzKeysScript writes the public keys, passed to it as $1, to the
55: 	// authorized_keys of every user in authzKeysTargets that exists.
56: 	authzKeysScript = `set -e
57: for entry in ` + authzKeysTargets + `; do
58: 	user=${entry%%:*}
59: 	home=${entry#*:}
60: 
61: 	` + authzKeysGuard + `
62: 
63: 	mkdir -p "$home/.ssh"
64: 	chmod 700 "$home/.ssh"
65: 	# The file is removed rather than truncated: a redirect onto an existing
66: 	# file keeps that file's ownership, and sshd rejects an authorized_keys
67: 	# that its user does not own.

69: 	printf '%s\n' "$1" > "$home/.ssh/authorized_keys"

151: func (n *frr) Init(cfg *clabtypes.NodeConfig, opts ...clabnodes.NodeOption) error {
152: 	// Init DefaultNode
153: 	n.DefaultNode = *clabnodes.NewDefaultNode(n)
154: 
155: 	n.Cfg = cfg
156: 	for _, o := range opts {
157: 		o(n)
158: 	}
159: 
160: 	// The whole directory is mounted rather than the three files individually.
161: 	// FRR saves a configuration by renaming frr.conf to frr.conf.sav and
162: 	// writing a new one, and a single-file bind mount cannot be renamed, so
163: 	// per-file mounts make every "write memory" report
164: 	//
165: 	//	Error renaming /etc/frr/frr.conf to /etc/frr/frr.conf.sav: Resource busy
166: 	//
167: 	// and lose the backup. Mounting the directory costs nothing: the image
168: 	// ships only daemons in /etc/frr, and containerlab writes that file too.
169: 	n.Cfg.Binds = append(n.Cfg.Binds,
170: 		fmt.Sprint(filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, cfgDir), ":", etcFRR),
171: 	)
172: 
173: 	// FRR programs routes into the kernel, which only forwards if asked to.
174: 	n.Cfg.Sysctls["net.ipv4.ip_forward"] = "1"
175: 	n.Cfg.Sysctls["net.ipv6.conf.all.forwarding"] = "1"
176: 
177: 	return nil
178: }

180: func (n *frr) PreDeploy(_ context.Context, params *clabnodes.PreDeployParams) error {
181: 	clabutils.CreateDirectory(n.Cfg.LabDir, clabconstants.PermissionsOpen)

184: 	if err != nil {
185: 		return err
186: 	}

194: func (n *frr) createFRRFiles() error {
195: 	nodeCfg := n.Config()
196: 
197: 	dir := filepath.Join(nodeCfg.LabDir, cfgDir)
198: 	clabutils.CreateDirectory(dir, clabconstants.PermissionsOpen)
199: 
200: 	// frr.conf comes from the user's startup-config when given, and from the
201: 	// bundled template otherwise. Routing it through GenerateConfig is what
202: 	// makes enforce-startup-config, suppress-startup-config and templating
203: 	// behave the same way here as for every other kind.
204: 	cfgTemplate := defaultCfgTemplate
205: 
206: 	if nodeCfg.StartupConfig != "" {
207: 		c, err := os.ReadFile(nodeCfg.StartupConfig)
208: 		if err != nil {
209: 			return err
210: 		}
211: 
212: 		cfgTemplate = string(c)
213: 	}

215: 	confPath := filepath.Join(dir, frrConfFile)
216: 
217: 	err := n.GenerateConfig(confPath, cfgTemplate)
218: 	if err != nil {
219: 		return fmt.Errorf("node=%s, failed to generate config: %w", nodeCfg.ShortName, err)
220: 	}
221: 
222: 	// GenerateConfig keeps an existing frr.conf, which may be one a previous
223: 	// "write memory" left owned by frr and unreadable, so repair it here too
224: 	// rather than only on save.
225: 	err = normalizeConfigFile(confPath)
226: 	if err != nil {
227: 		return fmt.Errorf("node=%s: %w", nodeCfg.ShortName, err)
228: 	}
229: 
230: 	rendered, err := renderDaemons(n.kindSpecificCfg().Daemons)
231: 	if err != nil {
232: 		return fmt.Errorf("node=%s: %w", nodeCfg.ShortName, err)
233: 	}
234:

236: 	if err != nil {
237: 		return fmt.Errorf("node=%s, failed to write daemons file: %w", nodeCfg.ShortName, err)
238: 	}
239:

241: 	if err != nil {
242: 		return fmt.Errorf("node=%s, failed to write vtysh.conf: %w", nodeCfg.ShortName, err)
243: 	}
244: 
245: 	return nil
246: }

248: // PostDeploy installs login credentials and keeps CLI-saved configs readable.
249: func (n *frr) PostDeploy(ctx context.Context, _ *clabnodes.PostDeployParams) error {
250: 	log.Debugf("Running postdeploy actions for frr %q node", n.Cfg.ShortName)
251: 
252: 	err := n.addSSHKeys(ctx)
253: 	if err != nil {
254: 		return err
255: 	}
256: 
257: 	err = n.setPassword(ctx)
258: 	if err != nil {
259: 		return err
260: 	}
261: 
262: 	return n.installConfigSaveWrapper(ctx)
263: }
264: 
265: func (n *frr) installConfigSaveWrapper(ctx context.Context) error {
266: 	uid, gid, err := clabutils.GetRealUserIDs()
267: 	if err != nil {
268: 		return err
269: 	}
270: 
271: 	// CHANGE: FRR can execute vtysh during PostDeploy. Keep its path available
272: 	// while preparing the original backup and atomically publishing the wrapper.
273: 	// Preserve the original backup across sequential PostDeploy calls.
274: 	script := `set -e
275: backup_tmp=''
276: wrapper_tmp=''
277: trap 'rm -f -- "$backup_tmp" "$wrapper_tmp"' EXIT
278: if [ ! -f "/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab" ]; then
279:     backup_tmp=$(mktemp "/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab.XXXXXX")
280:     cp -p "/usr/bin/vtysh" "$backup_tmp"
281:     mv -f -- "$backup_tmp" "/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab"
282: fi
283: wrapper_tmp=$(mktemp "/usr/bin/vtysh.wrapper.XXXXXX")
284: printf '%s\n' "$1" > "$wrapper_tmp"
285: chmod 755 "$wrapper_tmp"
286: mv -f -- "$wrapper_tmp" "/usr/bin/vtysh"`
287: 
288: 	wrapper := fmt.Sprintf(configSaveWrapper, uid, gid, clabconstants.PermissionsFileDefault)
289: 	cmd := clabexec.NewExecCmdFromSlice([]string{"bash", "-c", script, "--", wrapper})
290: 
291: 	result, err := n.RunExec(ctx, cmd)
292: 	if err != nil {
293: 		return fmt.Errorf(
294: 			"failed to install config save wrapper on node %q: %w",
295: 			n.Cfg.ShortName,
296: 			err,
297: 		)
298: 	}
299: 	if result.GetReturnCode() != 0 {
300: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to install config save wrapper on node %q: %s",
301: 			n.Cfg.ShortName, result.GetStdErrString())
302: 	}
303: 
304: 	return nil
305: }
306: 
307: // addSSHKeys adds the public keys containerlab collected from the host to the
308: // root and admin users' authorized_keys, to enable passwordless ssh. root gets
309: // a shell and admin gets vtysh, and both are reached with the same key.
310: func (n *frr) addSSHKeys(ctx context.Context) error {
311: 	if len(n.sshPubKeys) == 0 {
312: 		return nil
313: 	}
314: 
315: 	keys := strings.Join(clabutils.MarshalSSHPubKeys(n.sshPubKeys), "\n")
316: 
317: 	cmd := clabexec.NewExecCmdFromSlice(
318: 		[]string{"bash", "-c", authzKeysScript, "--", keys})
319: 
320: 	execResult, err := n.RunExec(ctx, cmd)
321: 	if err != nil {
322: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to add ssh keys to node %q: %w", n.Cfg.ShortName, err)
323: 	}
324: 
325: 	if execResult.GetReturnCode() != 0 {
326: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to add ssh keys to node %q: %s",
327: 			n.Cfg.ShortName, execResult.GetStdErrString())
328: 	}
329: 
330: 	return nil
331: }
332: 
333: // setPassword sets the password of the node's credentials user: admin with
334: // password admin, unless the topology's credentials say otherwise. The image
335: // ships admin without a password, so a well-known one is not baked into it;
336: // this is what makes the kind's default, or a topology's own credentials, work
337: // for a password login.
338: func (n *frr) setPassword(ctx context.Context) error {
339: 	user := n.Cfg.Credentials.Username
340: 	password := n.Cfg.Credentials.Password
341: 
342: 	if user == "" || password == "" {
343: 		return nil
344: 	}
345: 
346: 	// chpasswd reads one user:password pair per line, so a line break in the
347: 	// password would start a second pair, for whichever user it names.
348: 	if strings.ContainsAny(password, "\r\n") {
349: 		return fmt.Errorf("node %q: password must not contain a line break", n.Cfg.ShortName)
350: 	}
351: 
352: 	cmd := clabexec.NewExecCmdFromSlice(
353: 		[]string{"bash", "-c", passwordScript, "--", user, password})
354: 
355: 	execResult, err := n.RunExec(ctx, cmd)
356: 	if err != nil {
357: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to set password on node %q: %w", n.Cfg.ShortName, err)
358: 	}
359: 
360: 	if execResult.GetReturnCode() != 0 {
361: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to set password on node %q: %s",
362: 			n.Cfg.ShortName, execResult.GetStdErrString())
363: 	}
364: 
365: 	return nil
366: }
367: 
368: // normalizeConfigFile hands the node's frr.conf back to the user who ran
369: // containerlab. FRR writes that file as frr:frr mode 0600 whenever the node is
370: // asked to "write memory", and it is bind mounted, so the ownership lands on
371: // the lab directory copy and leaves the user unable to read their own saved
372: // configuration without root. Every other file containerlab puts in a lab
373: // directory goes through CreateFile, which does the same thing.
374: func normalizeConfigFile(path string) error {
375: 	// Nothing to fix up when the config was suppressed and never written.
376: 	if !clabutils.FileExists(path) {
377: 		return nil
378: 	}
379: 
380: 	err := clabutils.SetUIDAndGID(path)
381: 	if err != nil {
382: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to set ownership of %s: %w", path, err)
383: 	}
384: 
385: 	// Both os.WriteFile and os.Create apply a mode only when they create the
386: 	// file, so a 0600 that FRR left behind survives the write and has to be
387: 	// reset explicitly.
388: 	err = os.Chmod(path, clabconstants.PermissionsFileDefault)
389: 	if err != nil {
390: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to set permissions of %s: %w", path, err)
391: 	}
392: 
393: 	return nil
394: }
395: 
396: func (n *frr) SaveConfig(ctx context.Context) (*clabnodes.SaveConfigResult, error) {
397: 	cmd, _ := clabexec.NewExecCmdFromString(saveCmd)
398: 
399: 	execResult, err := n.RunExec(ctx, cmd)
400: 	if err != nil {
401: 		return nil, err
402: 	}
403: 
404: 	if execResult.GetReturnCode() != 0 {
405: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to save config on node %q: %s",
406: 			n.Cfg.ShortName, execResult.GetStdErrString())
407: 	}
408: 
409: 	confPath := filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, cfgDir, frrConfFile)
410: 
411: 	err = os.WriteFile(confPath, execResult.GetStdOutByteSlice(),
412: 		clabconstants.PermissionsFileDefault)
413: 	if err != nil {
414: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to write config by %s path from %s container: %w",
415: 			confPath, n.Cfg.ShortName, err)
416: 	}
417: 
418: 	err = normalizeConfigFile(confPath)
419: 	if err != nil {
420: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("node=%s: %w", n.Cfg.ShortName, err)
421: 	}
422: 
423: 	log.Infof("saved FRR configuration from %s node to %s\n", n.Cfg.ShortName, confPath)
424: 
425: 	// Reporting the path is what makes "containerlab save --copy" pick the file
426: 	// up. A nil result is how a kind says it cannot save at all, and copying is
427: 	// skipped for it without an error, so returning one here loses the config
428: 	// quietly.
429: 	return &clabnodes.SaveConfigResult{
430: 		ConfigPath: confPath,
431: 	}, nil
432: }

sshd\n\t// rejects an authorized_keys that user does not own.\n\tauthzKeysChown = `chown -R \"$user:\" \"$home/.ssh\"`\n\n\t// authzKeysScript writes the public keys, passed to it as $1, to the\n\t// authorized_keys of every user in authzKeysTargets that exists.\n\tauthzKeysScript = `set -e\nfor entry in ` + authzKeysTargets + `; do\n\tuser=${entry%%:*}\n\thome=${entry#*:}\n\n\t` + authzKeysGuard + `\n\n\tmkdir -p \"$home/.ssh\"\n\tchmod 700 \"$home/.ssh\"\n\t# The file is removed rather than truncated: a redirect onto an existing\n\t# file keeps that file's ownership, and sshd rejects an authorized_keys\n\t# that its user does not own.\n\trm -f \"$home/.ssh/authorized_keys\"\n\tprintf '%s\\n' \"$1\" > \"$home/.ssh/authorized_keys\"\n\tchmod 600 \"$home/.ssh/authorized_keys\"\n\t` + authzKeysChown + `\ndone`\n\n\t// passwordGuard skips a user the image does not have. A plain release image\n\t// has no admin, and root there is still reachable with the host's keys.\n\tpasswordGuard = `id -u \"$1\" >/dev/null 2>&1 || exit 0`\n\n\t// passwordScript sets the password of the user named by $1 to $2. Both\n\t// travel as arguments rather than in the script text, so no character in\n\t// the password can be read as shell.\n\tpasswordScript = passwordGuard + `\nprintf '%s:%s\\n' \"$1\" \"$2\" | chpasswd`\n\n\t// watchfrr invokes vtysh -w for CLI saves. Repair the bind-mounted file\n\t// before returning, while ordinary vtysh sessions still exec the binary.\n\tconfigSaveWrapper = `#!/bin/sh\ncase \"$*\" in\n    -w|--writeconfig)\n        \"/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab\" \"$@\" || exit $?\n        chown %d:%d \"/etc/frr/frr.conf\" || exit $?\n        chmod %04o \"/etc/frr/frr.conf\"\n        ;;\n    *)\n        exec \"/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab\" \"$@\"\n        ;;\nesac`\n\n\t// no-header keeps the \"Building configuration...\" preamble out of the\n\t// saved file, which is written back as the node's frr.conf.\n\tsaveCmd = `vtysh -c \"show running-config no-header\"`\n)\n\nvar (\n\t// defaultCredentials is the admin user the containerlab image ships, whose\n\t// login shell is vtysh. It is what \"ssh <node>\" uses, so a bare ssh to a\n\t// node reaches the routing CLI; \"ssh root@<node>\" still gets a shell. The\n\t// image has no password for admin: PostDeploy sets this one.\n\tdefaultCredentials = clabnodes.NewCredentials(\"admin\", \"admin\")\n\n\t//go:embed frr.cfg\n\tdefaultCfgTemplate string\n\n\t// vtyshCfg turns on integrated config so that frr.conf is the single\n\t// configuration file, matching what FRR ships upstream.\n\t//go:embed vtysh.conf\n\tvtyshCfg string\n)\n\n// KindSpecificConfig is the frr kind-specific config, set as keys on the node definition.\ntype KindSpecificConfig struct {\n\t// Daemons is the list of FRR routing daemons to enable. When empty, all\n\t// daemons known to the kind are enabled. The always-on daemons (zebra,\n\t// staticd, mgmtd, watchfrr) need not be listed.\n\tDaemons []string `yaml:\"daemons,omitempty\" json:\"daemons,omitempty\"`\n}\n\nvar kindSpecificConfig clabnodes.KindSpecificConfigSpec[KindSpecificConfig]\n\nfunc (n *frr) kindSpecificCfg() *KindSpecificConfig { return kindSpecificConfig.Of(n.Cfg) }\n\n// Register registers the node in the NodeRegistry.\nfunc Register(r *clabnodes.NodeRegistry) {\n\tgenerateNodeAttributes := clabnodes.NewGenerateNodeAttributes(generateable, generateIfFormat)\n\n\t// FRR has no scrapli or napalm platform, so no PlatformAttrs are set.\n\tnrea := clabnodes.NewNodeRegistryEntryAttributes(\n\t\tdefaultCredentials, generateNodeAttributes, nil,\n\t).WithKindSpecificConfig(kindSpecificConfig)\n\n\tr.Register(kindNames, func() clabnodes.Node {\n\t\treturn new(frr)\n\t}, nrea)\n}\n\ntype frr struct {\n\tclabnodes.DefaultNode\n\n\tsshPubKeys []ssh.PublicKey\n}\n\nfunc (n *frr) Init(cfg *clabtypes.NodeConfig, opts ...clabnodes.NodeOption) error {\n\t// Init DefaultNode\n\tn.DefaultNode = *clabnodes.NewDefaultNode(n)\n\n\tn.Cfg = cfg\n\tfor _, o := range opts {\n\t\to(n)\n\t}\n\n\t// The whole directory is mounted rather than the three files individually.\n\t// FRR saves a configuration by renaming frr.conf to frr.conf.sav and\n\t// writing a new one, and a single-file bind mount cannot be renamed, so\n\t// per-file mounts make every \"write memory\" report\n\t//\n\t//\tError renaming /etc/frr/frr.conf to /etc/frr/frr.conf.sav: Resource busy\n\t//\n\t// and lose the backup. Mounting the directory costs nothing: the image\n\t// ships only daemons in /etc/frr, and containerlab writes that file too.\n\tn.Cfg.Binds = append(n.Cfg.Binds,\n\t\tfmt.Sprint(filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, cfgDir), \":\", etcFRR),\n\t)\n\n\t// FRR programs routes into the kernel, which only forwards if asked to.\n\tn.Cfg.Sysctls[\"net.ipv4.ip_forward\"] = \"1\"\n\tn.Cfg.Sysctls[\"net.ipv6.conf.all.forwarding\"] = \"1\"\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc (n *frr) PreDeploy(_ context.Context, params *clabnodes.PreDeployParams) error {\n\tclabutils.CreateDirectory(n.Cfg.LabDir, clabconstants.PermissionsOpen)\n\n\t_, err := n.LoadOrGenerateCertificate(params.Cert, params.TopologyName)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\t// Recorded here and written to the container in PostDeploy, once it runs.\n\tn.sshPubKeys = params.SSHPubKeys\n\n\treturn n.createFRRFiles()\n}\n\nfunc (n *frr) createFRRFiles() error {\n\tnodeCfg := n.Config()\n\n\tdir := filepath.Join(nodeCfg.LabDir, cfgDir)\n\tclabutils.CreateDirectory(dir, clabconstants.PermissionsOpen)\n\n\t// frr.conf comes from the user's startup-config when given, and from the\n\t// bundled template otherwise. Routing it through GenerateConfig is what\n\t// makes enforce-startup-config, suppress-startup-config and templating\n\t// behave the same way here as for every other kind.\n\tcfgTemplate := defaultCfgTemplate\n\n\tif nodeCfg.StartupConfig != \"\" {\n\t\tc, err := os.ReadFile(nodeCfg.StartupConfig)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tcfgTemplate = CLI-saved configs readable.\nfunc (n *frr) PostDeploy(ctx context.Context, _ *clabnodes.PostDeployParams) error {\n\tlog.Debugf(\"Running postdeploy actions for frr %q node\", n.Cfg.ShortName)\n\n\terr := n.addSSHKeys(ctx)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\terr = n.setPassword(ctx)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\treturn CHANGE: FRR can execute vtysh during PostDeploy. Keep its path available\n\t// while preparing the original backup and atomically publishing the wrapper.\n\t// Preserve the original backup across sequential PostDeploy calls.\n\tscript := `set -e\nbackup_tmp=''\nwrapper_tmp=''\ntrap 'rm -f -- \"$backup_tmp\" \"$wrapper_tmp\"' EXIT\nif [ ! The image\n// ships admin without a password, so a well-known one is not baked into it;\n// this is what makes the kind's default, or a topology's own credentials, work\n// for a password login.\nfunc (n *frr) setPassword(ctx context.Context) error {\n\tuser := n.Cfg.Credentials.Username\n\tpassword := n.Cfg.Credentials.Password\n\n\tif user == \"\" || password == \"\" {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\t// chpasswd reads one user:password pair per line, so a line break in the\n\t// password would start a second pair, for whichever user it names.\n\tif strings.ContainsAny(password, \"\\r\\n\") {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"node %q: password must not contain a line break\", n.Cfg.ShortName)\n\t}\n\n\tcmd := clabexec.NewExecCmdFromSlice(\n\t\t[]string{\"bash\", \"-c\", passwordScript, \"--\", user, password})\n\n\texecResult, err := n.RunExec(ctx, cmd)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to set password on node %q: %w\", n.Cfg.ShortName, err)\n\t}\n\n\tif execResult.GetReturnCode() != 0 {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to set password on node %q: %s\",\n\t\t\tn.Cfg.ShortName, execResult.GetStdErrString())\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// normalizeConfigFile hands the node's frr.conf back to the user who ran\n// containerlab. FRR writes that file as frr:frr mode 0600 whenever the node is\n// asked to \"write memory\", and it is bind mounted, so the ownership lands on\n// the lab directory copy and leaves the user unable to read their own saved\n// configuration without root. Every other file containerlab puts in a lab\n// directory goes through CreateFile, which does the same thing.\nfunc normalizeConfigFile(path string) error {\n\t// Nothing to fix up when the config was suppressed and never written.\n\tif !clabutils.FileExists(path) {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\terr := clabutils.SetUIDAndGID(path)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to set ownership of %s: %w\", path, err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Both os.WriteFile and os.Create apply a mode only when they create the\n\t// file, so a 0600 that FRR left behind survives the write and has to be\n\t// reset explicitly.\n\terr = os.Chmod(path, clabconstants.PermissionsFileDefault)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to set permissions of %s: %w\", path, err)\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc (n *frr) SaveConfig(ctx context.Context) (*clabnodes.SaveConfigResult, error) {\n\tcmd, _ := clabexec.NewExecCmdFromString(saveCmd)\n\n\texecResult, err := n.RunExec(ctx, cmd)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\tif execResult.GetReturnCode() != 0 {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to save config on node %q: %s\",\n\t\t\tn.Cfg.ShortName, execResult.GetStdErrString())\n\t}\n\n\tconfPath := filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, cfgDir, frrConfFile)\n\n\terr = os.WriteFile(confPath, execResult.GetStdOutByteSlice(),\n\t\tclabconstants.PermissionsFileDefault)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to write config by %s path from %s container: %w\",\n\t\t\tconfPath, n.Cfg.ShortName, err)\n\t}\n\n\terr = normalizeConfigFile(confPath)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"node=%s: %w\", n.Cfg.ShortName, err)\n\t}\n\n\tlog.Infof(\"saved FRR configuration from %s node to %s\\n\", n.Cfg.ShortName, confPath)\n\n\t// Reporting the path is what makes \"containerlab save --copy\" pick the file\n\t// up. 25: // CHANGE: These interposers execute the real filesystem operations. Each probe models
26: // FRR starting vtysh between installer steps, without sleeps or production hooks.
27: const saveWrapperInterposers = `
28: frr_probe() {
29:     local status=0
30:     "$FRR_TEST_VTYSH" -b 'two words' > "$FRR_TEST_OUTPUT" 2>&1 || status=$?
31:     builtin printf '%s:%s\n' "$1" "$status" >> "$FRR_TEST_PROBES"
32: }
33: cp() {
34:     case "$FRR_TEST_FAULT" in
35:         copy|copy-interrupted)
36:             builtin printf 'partial copy' > "${@: -1}"
37:             [ "$FRR_TEST_FAULT" != copy-interrupted ] || kill -KILL $$
38:             return 91
39:             ;;
40:     esac
41:     command cp "$@" || return $?
42:     frr_probe copy
43: }
44: mv() {
45:     local stage=publish
46:     [ "${@: -1}" != "$FRR_TEST_VTYSH.containerlab" ] || stage=backup
47:     [ "$FRR_TEST_FAULT" != "$stage" ] || return 91
48:     command mv "$@" || return $?
49:     frr_probe "$stage"
50: }
51: printf() {
52:     if [ "$FRR_TEST_FAULT" = write ]; then
53:         builtin printf 'partial wrapper'
54:         return 91
55:     fi
56:     builtin printf "$@"
57: }
58: chmod() {
59:     [ "$FRR_TEST_FAULT" != chmod ] || return 91
60:     command chmod "$@" || return $?
61:     frr_probe chmod
62: }
63: `

67: 	configDir string
68: 	path      string
69: 	original  []byte

72: func newSaveWrapperFixture(t *testing.T) saveWrapperFixture {
73: 	t.Helper()
74: 
75: 	dir := filepath.Join(t.TempDir(), "with spaces")
76: 	f := saveWrapperFixture{
77: 		binDir:    filepath.Join(dir, "bin"),
78: 		configDir: filepath.Join(dir, "config"),
79: 		original:  []byte("#!/bin/sh\nprintf '%s\\n' \"$@\"\n"),
80: 	}
81: 	f.path = filepath.Join(f.binDir, "vtysh")
82: 	for _, path := range []string{f.binDir, f.configDir} {
83: 		if err := os.MkdirAll(path, 0o755); err != nil {
84: 			t.Fatal(err)
85: 		}
86: 	}
87: 
88: 	if err := os.WriteFile(f.path, f.original, 0o751); err != nil {
89: 		t.Fatal(err)
90: 	}
91:

93: 	t.Setenv("FRR_TEST_PROBES", filepath.Join(dir, "probes"))
94: 	t.Setenv("FRR_TEST_OUTPUT", filepath.Join(dir, "output"))
95: 	t.Setenv("FRR_TEST_FAULT", "")
96: 
97: 	return f
98: }

107: 		func(ctx context.Context, _ string, cmd *clabexec.ExecCmd) (*clabexec.ExecResult, error) {

211: func TestConfigSaveWrapperInstallationFailureAndRetry(t *testing.T) {
212: 	for _, fault := range []string{"copy", "copy-interrupted", "backup", "write", "chmod", "publish"} {
213: 		t.Run(fault, func(t *testing.T) {
214: 			f := newSaveWrapperFixture(t)
215: 			t.Setenv("FRR_TEST_FAULT", fault)
216: 			if err := f.install(t); err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), "router1") {
217: 				t.Fatalf("installation error = %v, want a node-specific failure", err)
218: 			}
219: 
220: 			f.assertCallable(t)
221: 			switch fault {
222: 			case "copy", "copy-interrupted", "backup":
223: 				if _, err := os.Stat(f.path + ".containerlab"); !os.IsNotExist(err) {
224: 					t.Fatalf("incomplete backup was published: %v", err)
225: 				}
226: 			default:
227: 				f.assertBackup(t)
228: 			}
229: 
230: 			// SIGKILL cannot run cleanup; abandoned temporary files must not
231: 			// become the original backup or interfere with the next attempt.
232: 			if fault != "copy-interrupted" {
233: 				f.assertNoTemporaryFiles(t)
234: 			}
235: 
236: 			leftovers := f.temporaryFiles(t)
237: 			if fault == "copy-interrupted" && leftovers == "" {
238: 				t.Fatal("interrupted-copy fixture did not leave a temporary copy")
239: 			}
240: 
241: 			t.Setenv("FRR_TEST_FAULT", "")
242: 			if err := f.install(t); err != nil {
243: 				t.Fatalf("retry failed: %v", err)
244: 			}
245: 
246: 			f.assertCallable(t)
247: 			f.assertBackup(t)
248: 			if fault != "copy-interrupted" {
249: 				f.assertNoTemporaryFiles(t)
250: 			} else if got := f.temporaryFiles(t); got != leftovers {
251: 				t.Errorf(
252: 					"retry changed abandoned temporary files: before=%q after=%q",
253: 					leftovers,
254: 					got,
255: 				)
256: 			}
257: 		})
258: 	}
259: }
260: 
261: func TestConfigSaveWrapperReinstallationFailure(t *testing.T) {
262: 	for _, fault := range []string{"write", "chmod", "publish"} {
263: 		t.Run(fault, func(t *testing.T) {
264: 			f := newSaveWrapperFixture(t)

266: 				t.Fatal(err)
267: 			}

270: 			if err != nil {
271: 				t.Fatal(err)
272: 			}
273: 
274: 			t.Setenv("FRR_TEST_FAULT", fault)
275: 			if err := f.install(t); err == nil {
276: 				t.Fatal("reinstallation unexpectedly succeeded")
277: 			}
278: 
279: 			after, err := os.ReadFile(f.path)
280: 			if err != nil || string(after) != string(before) {
281: 				t.Fatalf("failed reinstall changed the working wrapper: %v", err)
282: 			}
283: 
284: 			f.assertCallable(t)
285: 			f.assertBackup(t)
286: 			f.assertNoTemporaryFiles(t)
287: 		})
288: 	}
289: }

291: func TestConfigSaveWrapperPreservesOriginalMetadata(t *testing.T) {
292: 	f := newSaveWrapperFixture(t)
293: 	// Root can also exercise preservation of ownership different from the
294: 	// installer, as with the frrvty group in the container image.
295: 	if os.Geteuid() == 0 {
296: 		if err := os.Chown(f.path, 12345, 12346); err != nil {
297: 			t.Fatal(err)
298: 		}
299: 	}
300: 
301: 	// The image's BusyBox cp preserves timestamps at whole-second precision,
302: 	// which also matches the packaged executable's timestamp.
303: 	modified := time.Unix(1_700_000_000, 0)
304: 	if err := os.Chtimes(f.path, modified, modified); err != nil {
305: 		t.Fatal(err)
306: 	}
307: 
308: 	before, err := os.Stat(f.path)
309: 	if err != nil {
310: 		t.Fatal(err)
311: 	}
312: 
313: 	if err := f.install(t); err != nil {
314: 		t.Fatal(err)
315: 	}
316: 
317: 	after, err := os.Stat(f.path + ".containerlab")
318: 	if err != nil {
319: 		t.Fatal(err)
320: 	}
321: 
322: 	if before.Mode() != after.Mode() || !before.ModTime().Equal(after.ModTime()) {
323: 		t.Errorf("backup mode/time changed: before=%v %v after=%v %v",
324: 			before.Mode(), before.ModTime(), after.Mode(), after.ModTime())
325: 	}
326: 
327: 	oldStat, oldOK := before.Sys().(*syscall.Stat_t)
328: 	newStat, newOK := after.Sys().(*syscall.Stat_t)
329: 	if !oldOK || !newOK || oldStat.Uid != newStat.Uid || oldStat.Gid != newStat.Gid {
330: 		t.Error("backup ownership changed or could not be inspected")
331: 	}

Reasoning
Now let me examine the new script for correctness issues.

New script:
```sh
set -e
backup_tmp=''
wrapper_tmp=''
trap 'rm -f -- "$backup_tmp" "$wrapper_tmp"' EXIT
if [ ! -f "/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab" ]; then
    backup_tmp=$(mktemp "/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab.XXXXXX")
    cp -p "/usr/bin/vtysh" "$backup_tmp"
    mv -f -- "$backup_tmp" "/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab"
fi
wrapper_tmp=$(mktemp "/usr/bin/vtysh.wrapper.XXXXXX")
printf '%s\n' "$1" > "$wrapper_tmp"
chmod 755 "$wrapper_tmp"
mv -f -- "$wrapper_tmp" "/usr/bin/vtysh"
```

Potential issues:

1. mktemp template "vtysh.containerlab.XXXXXX" - the mktemp template must be the last component; XXXXXX at end - fine. Actually mktemp requires at least 3 X's at the end - fine.

2. `cp -p` then `mv` — the backup is published atomically. But wait: between the check `[ ! -f ... ]` and the cp, is there a race? Not within single-threaded script.

3. In base, `mv "/usr/bin/vtysh" "/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab"` — the original vtysh binary was moved. In head, cp -p creates a copy; the original `/usr/bin/vtysh` remains until the final `mv -f "$wrapper_tmp" "/usr/bin/vtysh"` replaces it atomically. Good — the change keeps vtysh path available during install.

4. Issue: the trap on EXIT removes tmp files. But if the script is killed (SIGKILL), tmp files remain — tests acknowledge that ("SIGKILL cannot run cleanup").

5. Potential correctness issue: `mktemp` on a path inside /usr/bin — mktemp creates file with 0600 by default. Then `cp -p` copies onto the existing file... wait, `cp -p src dest` where dest exists: cp overwrites the contents of dest? Actually `cp` opens dest with O_TRUNC and writes, preserving dest's mode/ownership? No — with `cp -p`, cp tries to preserve the source's mode/ownership/timestamps. When dest exists, cp opens it O_TRUNC|O_WRONLY and writes, then chmod/chown/utimes. So the final backup file has the source's mode (0755) and ownership. That's what `cp -p` gives. Fine.

But one subtlety: `cp -p` preserves ownership only if run as root; as non-root chown fails but cp -p doesn't fail on that? Actually GNU cp -p does not fail if chown fails (it just warns? Actually cp -p may exit with error if it can't preserve ownership? For `cp -p`, failure to preserve ownership results in a diagnostic but exit status... GNU cp: if chown fails and --preserve=ownership, it prints an error and exit status is 1? Let me recall: POSIX says cp -x... GNU cp: "if ownership cannot be preserved, no diagnostic is required unless --preserve=ownership" — with -p, GNU cp does report failure to chown? Actually GNU cp with -p will fail with exit status 1 if it cannot preserve ownership? Hmm. Testing: as non-root, `cp -p file owned-by-root /tmp/x` gives: "cp: preserving permissions for ..."? Actually I recall `cp -p` emits an error "cp: failed to preserve ownership" only for cross-filesystem? Not quite. Let me think: cp -p on a file you don't own, as non-root: chown fails with EPERM; GNU cp treats this as failure only if the user explicitly requested preservation? -p is explicit preservation. GNU cp returns nonzero? I believe GNU cp does return failure: "cp: preserving times/permissions/ownership of 'x': ..." Actually the message is "cp: failed to preserve ownership for 'x'" and it sets exit status... I recall it's a warning, not fatal, exit status 0? Configuration files — `frr.conf`, `daemons`, and `vtysh.conf` — are generated into the node's lab directory and bind-mounted over the container's `/etc/frr`, so `startup-config`, `enforce-startup-config`, and `suppress-startup-config` behave as they do for other kinds. Which daemons run is selected with `extras.frr.daemons`; when omitted, every daemon is started.

  Line 168: FRR publishes a [containerlab image](../manual/kinds/frr.md#getting-frrouting-image) alongside its plain release image, tagged `containerlab-<version>`. It adds an SSH server with an `admin` user whose login shell is `vtysh`, and removes the management network's default routes so they stay out of the lab's routing protocols. `clab save` writes the running configuration back to the lab directory.

Output

<path>/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

Output

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

Actually gomock default is `.Times(1)` if not specified? No — without .Times, the expectation defaults to exactly once (Times(1)). Wait: gomock's default: "If Times is not called, the mock is expected to be called exactly once"? Actually in gomock, an expectation without Times() is expected to be called exactly once. Hmm, no — I recall "An expectation without Times is expected exactly once" — yes, in gomock, if you don't specify times, it defaults to once. Actually let me check: `EXPECT().Exec(...)` — default Times is 1. Yes — install() creates a new controller each call, so each install has its own mock. Fine.

In frr_test.go's TestPostDeployConfigSaveWrapper, `.Times(2)` — fine, unchanged.

2. Check `newTestNode` in head frr_test.go line 27-40 for what it sets up (LabDir etc.) — not critical.

3. Now check the harness for a real potential issue: in the mock's DoAndReturn, `args[2]` is the script; `args[4]` is the wrapper. The replacement of `/usr/bin` in args[2] — the script also contains `/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab` etc. Fine. But note: the script text `mktemp "/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab.XXXXXX"` → becomes binDir path. Good.

But wait — there's a subtle issue with the interposers and the trap: the trap runs `rm -f -- "$backup_tmp" "$wrapper_tmp"`. `rm` is not interposed. Fine.

4. Potential issue in the interposers: `printf()` function shadows `printf` — but `frr_probe` uses `builtin printf`. However, the `cp` interposer uses `builtin printf 'partial copy' > "${@: -1}"` — that writes to the temp file even in the "copy" (non-interrupted) fault case, then returns 91. So the temp backup file contains "partial copy" and remains? No — in "copy" fault (not interrupted), after cp returns 91, set -e exits, trap removes backup_tmp. Test asserts no temp files for "copy". Good.

For "copy-interrupted": kill -KILL $$ kills the shell — but wait, the cp function runs in the current shell (function, not subshell), so `kill -KILL $$` kills the bash process. Then... does the EXIT trap run on SIGKILL? No. So backup_tmp remains. But hold on: the kill happens inside the function; but does bash even process the signal before the `return 91`? SIGKILL is immediate. The process dies with signal KILL. ExitError.ExitCode() returns -1. res.SetReturnCode(-1). installConfigSaveWrapper: result.GetReturnCode() != 0 → error. Good.

Hmm wait — one subtlety: `kill -KILL $$` — $$ is the main shell PID. When bash receives SIGKILL it dies immediately without running the trap. Good, matches test expectation.

But another subtlety: after `kill -KILL $$`, the following `return 91` never executes. Fine.

But actually — hmm, will `CombinedOutput` see the shell die with KILL? Yes.

5. Now, an important check: the wrapper script published as /usr/bin/vtysh — in production, `mv -f -- "$wrapper_tmp" "/usr/bin/vtysh"` replaces the original binary. But the original binary's file handle is gone; the original is preserved at /usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab. OK.

6. Now think about a real production gap: the `if [ ! -f "/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab" ]` check. Consider PostDeploy running twice concurrently on the same node (e.g., two containerlab processes or `deploy` racing). Base had the same race. Not worsened materially — actually with cp it's slightly better.

7. Now think about whether `mktemp` template with path works when /usr/bin doesn't allow... fine.

8. One more potential real issue: `printf '%s\n' "$1" > "$wrapper_tmp"` — wrapper contains `%d:%d` already formatted; no problem. But does wrapper contain any backslashes or `%`? configSaveWrapper contains `chmod %04o` — already substituted by fmt.Sprintf before being passed as $1. Fine.

9. Now let me reconsider the `cp -p` ownership issue more concretely. In the FRR containerlab image, /usr/bin/vtysh is owned by root with group frrvty maybe (per the test comment: "as with the frrvty group in the container image"). The script runs as root (container default). cp -p as root preserves ownership. Good.

But what if the exec runs as a non-root user? Docker exec without --user uses the image's default user (root for FRR). If the node sets `exec` user? RunExec — let me check how RunExec determines user. Probably uses default. Not worth flagging.

10. Now — is there an issue with `cp -p` when source and temp are on the same filesystem? No.

11. Now, the actual notable issue candidates:

(a) **Inode/atomicity of backup**: fine.

(b) **`cp -p` does not preserve xattrs/capabilities** (base `mv` did). vtysh binary — file capabilities? Unlikely; FRR's vtysh doesn't need caps. Also ACLs. This is a theoretical P4. Is it worth reporting? The instruction: return only supported, useful findings. It's a real semantic difference: previously `mv` moved the original file itself (preserving everything, including any xattrs); now the backup is a copy that preserves only mode/owner/timestamps. If vtysh had setuid or caps, they'd survive cp -p mode-wise (setuid is mode bit; caps are xattr). Actually cp -p preserves setuid/setgid bits. File capabilities would be lost. For vtysh, caps unlikely. I'd rate P4 and note it. Hmm, but is it "demonstrated impact"? It's speculative. Maybe better as coverage note. Let me think about whether there's a stronger correctness issue.

(c) **Disk space**: cp duplicates the binary — negligible.

(d) **The trap uses single quotes with variables expanded at execution — good.**

(e) **mktemp failure**: if /usr/bin is full or read-only, mktemp fails → set -e exits → trap rm fails silently. Error propagates via exit code. Fine.

(f) **Potential issue: `backup_tmp` template `vtysh.containerlab.XXXXXX` — mktemp requires at least 3 trailing X's — has 6. Good.

(g) **A subtle real bug**: `mv -f -- "$backup_tmp" "/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab"`. If a concurrent or leftover file exists named exactly `/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab` that is a *directory*, mv fails. Edge, skip.

(h) **Test issue**: `TestConfigSaveWrapperInstallationFailureAndRetry` for fault "copy-interrupted": after retry, it asserts `assertCallable` and `assertBackup` but intentionally doesn't assert temp files are gone; instead asserts they are unchanged. Fine, deliberate.

(i) **Test issue**: In the fault tests, the assertion `f.assertCallable(t)` right after a failed install: for fault "publish", the mv failed, so /usr/bin/vtysh is... the previous wrapper (or the original on first install). assertCallable runs f.path = the wrapper script → executes vtysh.containerlab → original → prints args. Good.

(j) **Wait, potential real bug in interposers**: The `mv` function: `[ "${@: -1}" != "$FRR_TEST_VTYSH.containerlab" ] || stage=backup`. But there are two mv calls: backup publish (`.../vtysh.containerlab`) and wrapper publish (`.../vtysh`). The wrapper publish's last arg is `f.path` which equals `"$FRR_TEST_VTYSH"`, not with .containerlab. So stage=publish. Good.

(k) **Probe on chmod**: chmod is called for wrapper_tmp. But also `cp -p` internally doesn't call chmod via shell. Good.

(l) **A real potential issue with the production script and `set -e` + trap**: If `cp` fails, set -e exits with cp's status. But `cp` returning 91 in test... in production fine.

(m) Now, what about the interposer's `printf` function and the probe? In `TestConfigSaveWrapperRemainsExecutableDuringInstall`, want includes "chmod:0" after "backup:0" — sequence: copy (cp), backup (mv), then wrapper: mktemp, printf (no probe), chmod (probe), publish (mv probe). So want = copy, backup, chmod, publish | chmod, publish. Matches.

(n) Now check the failure semantics of `RunExec` in the mock: on ExitError, SetStdErr(out) — the Go test uses CombinedOutput, so both streams go to "stderr". installConfigSaveWrapper error message includes stderr. Fine.

(o) Now, documentation: docs/manual/kinds/frr.md — does it describe the wrapper/backup? Probably not needed. The change is internal implementation; no user-visible behavior change (except resilience). No doc gap.

(p) Schema: no change needed.

(q) Now, one thing I want to verify: does the head script differ from base in that the backup is now a copy rather than a move — leaving `/usr/bin/vtysh` as the ORIGINAL file until the final mv. This is the point of the change: keep vtysh callable during install. Good.

(r) Another consideration — the `--` in `cp -p "/usr/bin/vtysh" "$backup_tmp"`? No `--` on cp, but paths are fixed constants, no injection. `mv -f --` used. Fine.

(s) Now think about **two PostDeploy calls racing within the same shell**: not applicable.

(t) **Idempotency with pre-existing temp backup from interrupted run**: The check is `[ ! -f "/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab" ]`. After an interrupted copy, `/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab` doesn't exist, so next run creates a NEW mktemp and leaves the old temp file. Over many interrupted attempts, temp files accumulate in /usr/bin (unbounded? only per interrupted attempt — rare). The test explicitly accepts leftovers for copy-interrupted. This matches the PR's intent. Could flag as P4: consider cleaning stale `vtysh.containerlab.*`/`vtysh.wrapper.*` temps on next run. Hmm, but that contradicts the test which asserts the retry does NOT change abandoned temp files ("retry changed abandoned temporary files: before=%q after=%q" — expects equal). So cleaning would break the test. The PR deliberately leaves them. Is leaving them a defect? They're 0600 partial files in /usr/bin; harmless but accumulate on repeated interruptions (rare). I could mention as P4. Actually wait — there's a subtle issue: the leftover backup temp file is named `vtysh.containerlab.XXXXXX` in /usr/bin — could anything glob `vtysh.containerlab*`? No.

(u) Now — a genuinely interesting one: **`chmod 755 "$wrapper_tmp"` uses numeric mode**; wrapper_tmp was created 0600 by mktemp; chmod 755 before mv. Between chmod and mv, the temp file is world-readable — fine.

(v) Now, one more: **the wrapper file is written via printf, and `printf` in production is the shell builtin** — no interposer in production. Good.

(w) **Bigger question — `mktemp` availability**: The FRR containerlab image is Debian-based (has coreutils). But the frr kind also works with "plain release images" per docs — PostDeploy runs on any frr kind node regardless of image. A minimal image (e.g., alpine-based FRR?) may lack GNU mktemp... BusyBox has mktemp. Alpine busybox mktemp supports template with suffix X's. Fine.

Also `cp -p` on BusyBox: supports -p. `mv -f --`: BusyBox mv supports `-f` and `--`? BusyBox getopt32 handles `--`. OK.

(x) Now — **the wrapper itself**: unchanged, references /usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab. Fine.

(y) Now let me double-check the probe file path assertion: probes written by `frr_probe` via `>> "$FRR_TEST_PROBES"`. Fine.

(z) **A potential real test bug**: In `install()`, the mock replaces "/usr/bin" in args[2] and args[4]. But `etcFRR` replacement in args[2]: the install script doesn't contain /etc/frr. Only the wrapper (args[4]) does. Harmless.

But wait — the wrapper (args[4]) also gets "/usr/bin" replaced → good.

Now — one more possible issue: `args[2] = saveWrapperInterposers + args[2]` — the interposers define functions, then the script. But the script begins with `set -e`. Order: functions defined first, then set -e. Good.

(aa) Now think about **whether the interposer `cp` function's probe ordering**: `command cp "$@" || return $?` then `frr_probe copy`. Good.

(bb) **Check `newTestNode`** at frr_test.go lines 27-40 to confirm LabDir etc. Not necessary.

Now, are there any genuine findings? Let me scrutinize the production script once more for a real bug.

```sh
set -e
backup_tmp=''
wrapper_tmp=''
trap 'rm -f -- "$backup_tmp" "$wrapper_tmp"' EXIT
if [ ! -f "/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab" ]; then
    backup_tmp=$(mktemp "/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab.XXXXXX")
    cp -p "/usr/bin/vtysh" "$backup_tmp"
    mv -f -- "$backup_tmp" "/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab"
fi
wrapper_tmp=$(mktemp "/usr/bin/vtysh.wrapper.XXXXXX")
printf '%s\n' "$1" > "$wrapper_tmp"
chmod 755 "$wrapper_tmp"
mv -f -- "$wrapper_tmp" "/usr/bin/vtysh"
```

Hmm — one thing: `rm -f -- "$backup_tmp" "$wrapper_tmp"` in the trap: if `rm` with empty vars — both initialized to '', so `rm -f -- "" ""`? With -f, rm ignores empty args? `rm -f -- ''` → "rm: cannot remove '': No such file or directory"? With -f, nonexistent files are ignored, but empty string... POSIX: rm -f shall not write diagnostics for nonexistent operands; an empty string operand — GNU rm treats '' as ENOENT and with -f suppresses. Yes, `rm -f ''` exits 0 silently. Good.

Another: **exit status preservation through trap**: bash preserves the original exit status when the trap's last command succeeds... Actually if the trap runs `rm` which succeeds, exit status is preserved (bash: exit status of script is the status at exit, unless the trap calls exit). Good.

Now — one real subtlety: **`cp -p` on a file being executed**. /usr/bin/vtysh might be executing (an active vtysh session) while cp reads it. Reading an executable while running is fine on Linux.

Another: **what if /usr/bin/vtysh is busy (text file busy) for the final mv?** mv (rename) doesn't need write; ETXTBSY only applies to open-for-write on running executable. The original base had `mv` too, and the wrapper tmp write was a different file. Actually with base: `mv -f vtysh.containerlab.tmp /usr/bin/vtysh` — rename over a running executable is fine. Same here. Good.

Another real consideration: **`cp -p` failing with exit status due to inability to preserve ownership when exec user is non-root.** Let me pin down GNU cp behavior: From coreutils source, when chown fails during copy with preserve_ownership, cp calls... In copy.c, after set-owner fails, it records "cannot preserve ownership" and if the user requested preservation explicitly (-p), it emits an error diagnostic and sets copy failure → exit status 1? Actually I recall: "cp: cannot preserve ownership" is only reported when the file's ownership differs and --preserve=ownership; and it does cause exit failure? Let me recall empirically: as a normal user, `cp -p /usr/bin/ls /tmp/x` — succeeds? /usr/bin/ls owned by root, copying to /tmp/x as user: chown fails (EPERM). GNU cp: this is NOT an error; it silently skips preserving ownership? Hmm. I believe cp only warns "preserving permissions" failures... From coreutils docs: "cp -p: preserve ... but fail if unable"? The POSIX spec says cp -p shall fail if it cannot preserve ownership? POSIX: "If the -p option is specified... if any of the preservation fails, cp shall write a diagnostic... and exit with non-zero status"? POSIX says: "if preservation of any of these fails for any reason, cp shall write a diagnostic message to stderr, but shall cause no further modification of the destination file... and exit with non-zero status"? Actually POSIX: "If the -p option was specified, and preservation of file owner/group fails, it is not considered an error if the copy is owned by the current user..." Hmm, I don't remember precisely.

Real-world: `cp -p` as non-root on a root-owned file — I'm fairly confident this succeeds (exit 0) in GNU coreutils and BusyBox, silently preserving what it can (mode, timestamps it can; ownership left as the copying user if chown fails and source owner == current user it's fine; else it warns?). GNU coreutils copy.c: when chown fails with EPERM, if the ownership is being changed to something different from the current, it emits an error UNLESS the failure is because of non-root and... I recall "cp: preserving permissions for..." warnings only for SELinux contexts. For plain ownership: if the copy's uid != source uid after chown failure, cp treats it as failure ONLY when the current user isn't the source's owner... This is getting deep. In practice, PostDeploy exec in container runs as the container's default user (root for FRR images; the frr kind expects root for chown anyway — note the wrapper does `chown` on frr.conf, which requires root). So non-root exec is already unsupported for this feature. Not a finding.

Now, is there anything about **`mv -f` on a read-only bind mount**? No.

Let me now consider the **test file's `for range 2`** — Go 1.22+ range-over-int. Check the repo's go version — containerlab uses recent Go. Fine.

Now potential **golangci-lint issue**: `for _, fault := range []string{...}` with `t.Run(fault, ...)` and `t.Setenv` inside subtests — t.Setenv in subtests is fine (no parallel). Fine.

Now — one genuine issue I see: **the mock in the new test registers `.DoAndReturn` without `.Times()`, and `install()` is called twice in the first test but within one `install` call the Exec happens once** — each install creates a new controller. OK.

Another: **`clabmocksmockruntime.NewMockContainerRuntime(gomock.NewController(t))`** — when install returns an error (fault tests), the controller checks expectations at Cleanup — no unfulfilled expectations since DoAndReturn was called once. Fine.

Now, let me reconsider a subtle behavioral difference worth flagging: **the backup is now a copy of the original binary, but the original `/usr/bin/vtysh` remains a distinct file that gets replaced by the wrapper. In base, `/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab` WAS the original inode.** Any consequence? Docker image layer diffs? No. Overlayfs copy-up: mv within same upper layer... fine.

But note: the `printf` function is also called by the INSTALL script only once. However — wait, there's a trap here: `printf` is shadowed, and `mktemp` output isn't affected. Fine.

Now — an important detail: in the fault=write case, the script's stdout redirect `> "$wrapper_tmp"` truncates the temp file created by mktemp (0600). Then set -e exits; trap removes. assertNoTemporaryFiles passes. Good.

Now — potential real-world concern I should verify: **does `mktemp` exist in the FRR containerlab image?** The frr kind is used with both the containerlab image and plain release images. Debian-based FRR images include coreutils (mktemp, cp, mv). Fine.

Let me now think about whether there's any issue with the **`trap` clearing both tmps each time** — after successful mv, backup_tmp is empty string (renamed), so rm -f on a nonexistent path — wait: after `mv "$backup_tmp" target`, $backup_tmp still holds the old temp path which no longer exists; rm -f is fine.

Now, one more possible real defect: **the second PostDeploy's `wrapper_tmp` name** — fine.

Now consider **concurrent PostDeploy for the same node** (e.g., `containerlab deploy -r` while another deploy runs) — the check-then-act between `[ ! -f ]` and mktemp could interleave: both create temps, both cp, both mv -f — second mv overwrites with identical content. Benign.

Now, the **frr.go script's `bash -c script -- wrapper`**: `bash -c script -- wrapper` means $0=`--`, $1=wrapper. Good, unchanged pattern.

Now — the CHANGE comment mentions "FRR can execute vtysh during PostDeploy" — watchfrr invokes vtysh -w during startup, which races with PostDeploy. In base, between `mv vtysh vtysh.containerlab` and the final `mv -f tmp vtysh`, /usr/bin/vtysh didn't exist → watchfrr's vtysh invocation would fail. Head fixes it. Good — that's the purpose.

Wait — but there's still a window in head: after the final `mv -f -- "$wrapper_tmp" "/usr/bin/vtysh"`, /usr/bin/vtysh is the wrapper — fine, wrapper forwards. During install, /usr/bin/vtysh is the original. So vtysh is always available. 

Now — is there any window where `/usr/bin/vtysh` is MISSING in head? No: cp leaves the original in place; only the final mv replaces it atomically. Good.

But hold on — what about the very first install on a **fresh container where a previous base-version install already ran**? Not relevant.

Now, one potential issue: **`cp -p` when the source `/usr/bin/vtysh` has multiple hard links**? cp copies content; hardlink count of original elsewhere in the image (e.g., busybox links)? vtysh is its own binary. Fine.

But if it were a symlink, `cp -p` follows it (copies target content) whereas `mv` renames the symlink. Then the backup would be a regular file instead of a symlink — wrapper would exec the real binary content — still functional. Slight difference; base `mv` preserved the symlink. # Code Review: `frr` config-save wrapper installation hardening

## Scope

This PR changes two files:

- [`nodes/frr/frr.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/7d2236b24bf6dcdd313993b4d8b4029e6d2a18e3/nodes/frr/frr.go#L265-L305) — rewrites the `installConfigSaveWrapper` install script: the original `vtysh` binary is now backed up with `cp -p` into a `mktemp` file and published with an atomic `mv`, so `/usr/bin/vtysh` stays callable while the backup and wrapper are prepared; temporary files are cleaned by an `EXIT` trap.
- [`nodes/frr/frr_save_wrapper_test.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/7d2236b24bf6dcdd313993b4d8b4029e6d2a18e3/nodes/frr/frr_save_wrapper_test.go#L1-L332) (new) — fault-injection and concurrency tests for the installer script.

## Summary of findings

| Priority | Count | Titles |
| --- | --- | --- |
| P0 | 0 | — |
| P1 | 0 | — |
| P2 | 0 | — |
| P3 | 0 | — |
| P4 | 2 | `cp -p` backup drops attributes that the base `mv` preserved; `CHANGE:` comment markers are review-session artifacts |

The core change is correct. I traced the script end to end: the `[ ! -f /usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab ]` check is unchanged, so re-install does not wrap the wrapper (`TestPostDeployWrapper` in `frr_test.go` still covers that). The backup is published by `mv` from a `mktemp` file, so a partially copied backup can never appear at `/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab`; the wrapper is likewise published by atomic rename after it is fully written and chmodded. On the happy path `/usr/bin/vtysh` is never absent — `cp` leaves the original in place until the final `mv -f -- "$wrapper_tmp" "/usr/bin/vtysh"` replaces it by rename, which fixes the base window where `watchfrr`-spawned `vtysh -w` would have hit a missing binary. The `EXIT` trap cleans both temporaries, and `mktemp`/`cp -p`/`mv -f --`/`chmod` are all available in the Debian-based FRR images (and BusyBox equivalents where present). The test harness correctly rewrites the `/usr/bin` and `etcFRR` placeholders in both the script (`args[2]`) and the wrapper (`args[4]`), and every fault path (`copy`, `copy-interrupted`, `backup`, `write`, `chmod`, `publish`) asserts the right post-state, including that a `SIGKILL`ed copy leaves no published backup.

## Findings

### P4 — `cp -p` backup loses attributes that the base `mv` preserved verbatim

**Location:** [`nodes/frr/frr.go`, lines 271-282](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/7d2236b24bf6dcdd313993b4d8b4029e6d2a18e3/nodes/frr/frr.go#L271-L282)

**Problem:** The base script moved the original binary itself (`mv "/usr/bin/vtysh" "/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab"`), so the backup was the same inode with every attribute intact. The new script copies it with `cp -p`, which preserves only mode, ownership, and timestamps. Extended attributes — file capabilities (`security.capability`), ACLs, and SELinux labels — are not preserved by `cp -p` (GNU `cp` needs `--preserve=all`/`-a`), and a symlinked `vtysh` would additionally be dereferenced into a regular file.

**Trigger:** Only if a distribution's `vtysh` binary carries file capabilities, ACLs, or is a symlink. The current FRR containerlab and release images do not set capabilities on `vtysh`, so this is latent, not demonstrated on today's images.

**Impact:** The wrapper `exec`s `/usr/bin/vtysh.containerlab`, so any lost capability or ACL would apply to every wrapped `vtysh -w` invocation on such an image.

**Correction:** Change `cp -p` to `cp -a` (a one-token change that also preserves xattrs) or, if the reduction is accepted deliberately, mark it with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling as the repository's rules require for known tradeoffs.

### P4 — `CHANGE:` comment prefixes are review-session artifacts, not durable comments

**Location:** [`nodes/frr/frr.go`, lines 271-273](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/7d2236b24bf6dcdd313993b4d8b4029e6d2a18e3/nodes/frr/frr.go#L271-L273) and [`nodes/frr/frr_save_wrapper_test.go`, lines 25-26](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/7d2236b24bf6dcdd313993b4d8b4029e6d2a18e3/nodes/frr/frr_save_wrapper_test.go#L25-L26)

**Problem:** The comments added by this PR begin with `CHANGE:`, which reads as a note addressed to a specific review iteration rather than documentation for future readers. The underlying content is otherwise good — for example, "FRR can execute vtysh during PostDeploy. Keep its path available while preparing the original backup" is exactly the durable rationale the code needs.

**Trigger:** Every future reader of `installConfigSaveWrapper` or the test file encounters the marker with no change to relate it to once the PR merges.

**Impact:** Minor reader confusion and comment churn in future diffs; no functional effect.

**Correction:** Drop the `CHANGE:` prefixes and keep the explanatory text as-is, both in `frr.go` and in `frr_save_wrapper_test.go`.

## Coverage notes

- Coverage is source-only: I inspected the changed script, both revisions of `nodes/frr/frr.go`, the new test file, the pre-existing `TestPostDeployConfigSaveWrapper` in `nodes/frr/frr_test.go`, `docs/manual/kinds/frr.md` (no mention of the wrapper internals, so no doc update is required for this internal fix), and `schemas/clab.schema.json` (no topology API surface is touched, so no schema change applies). I did not execute the tests or run the container flow, so test-passing and runtime behavior are unverified; this review is based on static analysis.
- Dependencies check out: `github.com/google/go-cmp/cmp` is already a direct dependency in `go.mod`, and `go.uber.org/mock/gomock`, `SetReturnCode`/`SetStdErr`/`NewExecResult` all match existing usage in `exec/exec.go` and other node tests. `go 1.26.0` supports the `for range 2` loop.
- Leftover `vtysh.containerlab.XXXXXX` / `vtysh.wrapper.XXXXXX` files in `/usr/bin` after a `SIGKILL`ed install are accepted by design and asserted by the retry test; they accumulate only across interrupted attempts and never become the backup, so I did not raise a finding for them.
- The pre-existing hazard that a missing `vtysh.containerlab` alongside an already-wrapped `vtysh` would back up the wrapper (leading to wrapper self-execution) exists identically in the base and was not worsened by this PR; it is not reported as a PR defect.
- No release-note entry was added. The `.cursor/rules/release-note-review.mdc` rule only mandates inspection when the PR changes files under `docs/rn/`, which this one does not, so I did not raise a finding; maintainers may still want a note in the next unreleased release notes for the concurrency fix to the 0.80-era `frr` kind.
